Mr. President, valued lawmakers, one of the most effective ways to conduct tender transactions effectively, transparently and quickly, reduce transaction costs, participate in tenders and increase competition is to implement the electronic tender system. In this sense, the Electronic Public Import Platform was gradually activated as of September 2010. The administrations are able to prepare the tender document through the EKAP through the intelligent program that controls compliance with legislation. The tender ads are automatically created by the system. So the longest tender period of 40 days is actually reduced to 28 days. Increase the annual number of ads in the Public Contraction bulletin approximately 339. So far, the savings of the EKAP system is over $3 billion. Downloading the tender documents over the EKAP is equivalent to preventing the cutting of 7,700 trees. This savings is expected to increase further in the future. The ECAP contributes to boosting competition in tenders. There is a chance to participate in tenders from anywhere in the country, the furthest part of the country, to receive documents. Financial reporting from independent control has been enforced by the new Turkish Trade Law for specific businesses. The company's consolidation system is also removed and the independent control system is adopted. Globalization and technological developments have increased the mobility of capital markets in modern times. Today, many foreign companies are being processed in many markets around the world. This has also made it necessary for reliable financial information to be submitted to investors in international standards. Based on untrustworthy information, investments can do great harm to society and to decisionmakers. In the past, painful examples of this have occurred worldwide. From the beginning of the 2000s, in the United States of America and in other developed countries, many major companies have lost their investors due to false reports, and they have failed themselves. This has severely damaged the credibility of those involved in the process of drafting and overseeing financial tables. In this regard, investors and decisionmakers seek independent security in measuring financial information. In particular, companies operating in more than one country face different regulations, different accounting practices. This has led to the need to adapt to the financial reporting arrangement. Mr. President, the high-profile lawmakers have further demonstrated the importance of accounting and financial information with the approach of institutional management and global economic crises from time to time. Many developed countries, particularly the United States, have had to take stricter measures to protect the individual investor and increase the credibility of the financial reporting process. The most concrete and common of these measures is the tightening of controls on accounting and control and the establishment of the public oversight system in this area. Public custody over independent control has been established, provided the services related to accounting and audit are given to state-run oversight institutions. Independent control, in this sense, is a key factor in the development of an administrative and financially sustainable structure. One of the main priorities of EC PARTY governments is the growth of the economy through the private sector. Public authority is responsible for taking all necessary measures to protect the investor and ensure that the markets function in a healthy manner. In this regard, it is vital that the control mechanisms are shaped in line with the development of the economic structure and overcome the old sense of control. The most important development in this period in terms of the institution is the setting of the criteria of companies that will be subject to independent control by the Council of Ministers' decision for 2013. tbmm-2013-12-17sit06spe0420par0135	I'm reading the reason: As in the budgets of the past year, "2014 Central Management Budget Law Bill (6. Article 5018 and Constitution 161 and 163 are the powers vested in the Minister of Finance. In addition to violating Articles 5018, it renders the transparency and accountability-based financial system which the Law stipulated, and also causes "expensive spending" in violation of Law 5018. The basis of Law 5018 is "strategic planning and performance-based budgeting," and the budgets of public administrations are designed as a holistic process that is interconnected and complements each other, such as the strategic plan-performance program-budget, and forms a hierarchical structure from the top down. The Fiscal System 5018 has been structured on performance basis, and, for example, the transfer of the grant to the "pre-school education" operation in the Ministry of National Education budget by the Finance Minister to the DSI General Directorate's "sweet water fishing operation" is failing to implement the strategic plan-performance-based performance-based budgeting system based on its strategic plan-performance programme-budget, and it is expected that public resources will be effective, economic, efficient and accountable. Because the funding for the Ministry of Education budget's "pre-school education" operation was set up to achieve a specific goal in the National Ministry of Education's strategic plan and vision. The transfer of the grant to the DSI's "sugar water fishing" operation has led to the failure of the ministry's expected benefit from "pre-school education" and the failure to assess the performance of the "pre-school education" activities, while the DSI General Directorate's budget has no aim and no predetermined performance indicators to assess the benefit of its resources. This applies to the Finance Minister without exception for all grants provided to him under budget laws. According to Law 5018, all funds set up in the public administration budget and dedicated to activities and projects must be obtained from the performance programme and target and indicators predetermined. The transfer of funds to specific public administration budgets and projects to other institutions, the performance-seeking budgets of the administrations that have been funneled funds, while the public administrations where the funds are being transferred have no idea of what they're doing, and they have no idea of why they're doing it, and they have no idea of what they're doing it in their budgets. As a result, the strategic planning and performance-based budgetary system, envisioned by Law 5018, is invalidated by the authority vested in budget laws by the Minister of Finance.
